=== PAGE 1 ===
Death Avoidance as the Dominant Routing Signal:
A Counterfactual Analysis on ScienceWorld
Anonymous ACL submission
001
Abstract
002
003
004
Modular LLM agents that route between neural reasoning and deterministic computation
promise improved reliability, yet understanding
what drives the value of routing decisions remains unclear. We propose episode-level counterfactual analysis: running co
under pure strategies and comparing final outcomes to decompose routing signal into deathavoidance and non-death compone
task types exhibiting meaningful routing signal in prescreening (11 of 30 available ScienceWorld task types, 245 episode
(75 of 95) are driven by death asymmetry—
a penalty-independent metric. Under the default penalty P =100, the Death Avoidance Factor (DAF) is 90.4% (95% CI: [81.6
though DAF varies from 50% (P =10) to 98%
(P =500) across penalty settings (Appendix F).
Per-task death rate correlates strongly with routing signal (r=0.97, though partly structural
as death penalties contribute directly to score
differences; §3.1). An exploratory step-level
router trained on these labels achieves 54.1 ±
5.4% step-level balanced accuracy (with sameepisode leakage) but only 47.1 ± 2.2% on heldout episodes (below the always-
baseline of 50%); data scaling (32×) and capacity scaling (0.6B–4B) confirm this is structural
rather than data-limited. Cross-environment
analysis on MATH (N =300) shows routing signal vanishes without a death penalty: the label
distribution inverts from 86:9 (deterministicpreferred) to 30:17 (internal directionally favored), with a directional tr
0.079, not reaching α=0.05). A Llama-3.18B pilot provides preliminary directional evidence that death-driven routing gen
across model families. These findings indicate
that in environments with catastrophic failure
modes, routing value is dominated by safety—
the router functions primarily as a safety critic
rather than an efficiency optimizer.

Ethics Statement
541

This work uses ScienceWorld (Wang et al., 2022),
a publicly available text-based simulation environment, and does not involve human subjects or private data. All languag
standard APIs or publicly released weights. We report computational costs: approximately 40 GPUhours on two consumer GPU
router training, and evaluation. Code and data will
be released upon acceptance.

We introduced episode-level counterfactual analysis for decomposing routing signal in modular LLM
agents. Applied to 245 ScienceWorld episodes (11
of 30 task types, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), 78.9% of
non-tie routing labels (75 of 95) arise from death
asymmetry. The DAF reaches 90.4% (95% CI:
[81.6%, 98.8%]) at P =100, with strong cross-task
correlation (r = 0.97, p < 10−6 ).
Cross-environment evidence supports this finding: on MATH (N =300), routing signal vanishes
without death penalty, with a directional trend favoring internal reasoning (+4.3 pp, p = 0.079).
An exploratory router trained on counterfactual
labels achieves only 47.1 ± 2.2% on held-out
episodes—below always-deterministic—a structural limitation confirmed by data and capacity
scaling ablations.
In environments with catastrophic failure modes,

[Director 指令] [Patch] Round 8 审稿修复 — 全部文字修改，Reviewer 已审查通过。请逐条执行：

**R8-W1 (Must-Fix): "vanishes" 替换**
全文搜索 "vanishes"/"vanish"/"routing signal vanishes"。替换为：
"the death-driven routing signal is not statistically detectable in MATH (McNemar p=0.079, not reaching α=0.05)"
确保 Conclusion 中也有 "not reaching α=0.05" hedge。

**R8-W2 (Should-Fix): Data scaling minority-class confound**
Appendix E 中找到 "rules out data scarcity"，软化为：
"rules out step-level data scarcity; episode-level minority-class diversity remains a potential confound that cannot be resolved without collecting more internal-preferred episodes. The ground-truth internal-preferred rate (9/245 = 3.7%) means collecting 50 internal-preferred episodes would require approximately 1,350 paired episodes at current environment dynamics—a 5.5× expansion with diminishing return on a 9.6% signal."

**R8-W4 (Should-Fix): "Safety critic" 类比**
Discussion 中找到 "router functions as a safety critic"，替换为：
"The routing problem reduces to safety-first modality selection—closer to a conservative default policy than to a learned safety critic."

**R8-W5 (Presentation): Modality 异质性**
Discussion 中新增 1 段。**必须先说 why 再列 what**：
"The deliberate heterogeneity in deterministic modality definition—ground-truth state observation in ScienceWorld, code execution in MATH, admissible-action filtering in ALFWorld—tests whether the framework (episode-level counterfactual analysis) generalizes, not whether any single modality definition does. The consistent finding across structurally different modalities strengthens rather than weakens the conclusion that catastrophic-failure avoidance is the dominant routing signal wherever it exists."

**R8-W7 (Minor): r=0.97 数学近必然性**
§4.1 首次提到 r=0.97 时，加：
"A high correlation is expected whenever the scaled death-rate differential (P × Δ_d) dominates between-task variance—formally, when Var(P·Δ_d) ≫ Var(Δ_nd). At P=100, this condition holds for all but one task type (find-non-living-thing, which has zero deaths). The sensitivity analysis (Appendix D) confirms that r drops below significance at P≤25, where this dominance condition no longer holds."

**Q3 补充句**
Discussion 合适位置加一句：
"In principle, routing could be fully reduced to per-task-type death-risk prediction, bypassing step-level observation entirely—a direction we leave for future work."

**Reviewer 确认的两个已处理项**：
- ALFWorld DAF tautology：你之前确认论文中没有 ALFWorld DAF 数字，无需改。
- Penalty sensitivity P≤25：你之前已写入 Appendix（threshold effect section），无需改。

R8-W3（capacity scaling episode-level）和 R8-W6（reasoning depth n）等 Agent 回数据后再补。R8-m1（bib cleanup）也等 Agent 确认后处理。

以上 5 项改完后汇报。

_source line 2496 · 2026-05-16T12:42:25.965541+00:00_

1	\begin{abstract}
2	Modular LLM agents that route between neural reasoning and deterministic computation promise improved reliability, yet understanding \emph{what} drives the value of routing decisions remains unclear. We propose episode-level counterfactual analysis: running complete episodes under pure strategies and comparing final outcomes to decompose routing signal into death-avoidance and non-death components. Among task types exhibiting meaningful routing signal in prescreening (11 of 30 available ScienceWorld task types, 245 episodes, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), 78.9\% of non-tie routing labels (75 of 95) are driven by death asymmetry---a penalty-independent metric. Under the default penalty $P{=}100$, the Death Avoidance Factor (DAF) is $90.4\%$ (95\% CI: $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$), though DAF varies from 50\% ($P{=}10$) to 98\% ($P{=}500$) across penalty settings (Appendix~\ref{app:penalty}). Per-task death rate correlates strongly with routing signal ($r{=}0.97$, though partly structural as death penalties contribute directly to score differences; \S\ref{sec:death-decomp}). An exploratory step-level router trained on these labels achieves $54.1 \pm 5.4\%$ step-level balanced accuracy (with same-episode leakage) but only $47.1 \pm 2.2\%$ on held-out episodes (below the always-deterministic baseline of 50\%); data scaling ($32{\times}$) and capacity scaling (0.6B--4B) confirm this is structural rather than data-limited. Cross-environment analysis on MATH ($N{=}300$) shows routing signal vanishes without a death penalty: the label distribution inverts from 86:9 (deterministic-preferred) to 30:17 (internal directionally favored), with a directional trend favoring internal reasoning ($+4.3$ percentage points, $p = 0.079$, not reaching $\alpha{=}0.05$). A Llama-3.1-8B pilot provides preliminary directional evidence that death-driven routing generalizes across model families. These findings indicate that in environments with catastrophic failure modes, routing value is do

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Cross-environment evidence.}
5	Does routing signal persist without catastrophic failure? On MATH Level~4--5 (300 problems), using a code interpreter as the deterministic modality, internal reasoning directionally outperforms: 81.0\% vs.\ 76.7\% accuracy ($+4.3$~pp, 95\% CI $[0.0, +8.7]$; McNemar $p = 0.079$, not reaching $\alpha{=}0.05$). The label distribution inverts: 30 internal-preferred vs.\ 17 deterministic-preferred (cf.\ 9:86 in ScienceWorld), with 84.3\% no-preference. In the absence of death risk, the deterministic advantage vanishes---consistent with death avoidance as the primary driver. A complementary evaluation on ALFWorld ($N{=}135$) confirms that the counterfactual framework identifies different routing mechanisms across environments: the deterministic advantage there stems from information asymmetry (access to admissible actions) rather than death avoidance (Appendix~\ref{app:cross-env}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Penalty sensitivity.}
8	The $r = 0.97$ correlation is specific to catastrophic death (score $= -100$); defining ``failure'' as any non-positive score yields $r = -0.13$ ($p = 0.70$). The correlation emerges only at $P \geq 50$ ($r = 0.86$) and reflects that non-death episodes show near-zero performance differential ($\Delta_{\text{nd}} = 2.84$; Appendix~\ref{app:penalty}). Death is effectively the \emph{only} component generating differential routing signal in ScienceWorld. These findings may be capability-dependent: the 42.9\% internal death rate reflects a 7B model's hallucination vulnerability; larger models with lower death rates would reduce DAF, potentially revealing non-death routing signal currently masked by catastrophic failures.
9	
10	\paragraph{Implications for router design.}
11	The dominance of death avoidance (DAF $= 90.4\%$ at $P{=}100$) reframes modality routing as primarily a \emph{safety} problem rather than an efficiency-quality tradeoff. The router functions as a s
…[truncated 663 chars]

1	\section{Experiments}
2	
3	\subsection{Death Avoidance Decomposition}
4	\label{sec:death-decomp}
5	
6	\begin{figure}[t]
7	\centering
8	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig1_daf_scatter.pdf}
9	\caption{Per-task scatter: death rate differential (internal $-$ deterministic) vs.\ performance gap ($\Delta_{\text{full}}$). $r = 0.97$, $p < 10^{-6}$, $n = 11$ task types.}
10	\label{fig:decomposition}
11	\end{figure}
12	
13	Across 245 episodes, $\Delta_{\text{full}} = +29.60$; excluding death episodes, $\Delta_{\text{nd}} = +2.84$, yielding DAF $= 90.4\%$ (95\% CI: $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$; at default $P{=}100$). The penalty-independent death-asymmetry label fraction is 78.9\% (75 of 95 non-tie labels). The internal strategy dies in 42.9\% of episodes versus 16.3\% for deterministic (2.6$\times$). Per-task, death rate differential correlates strongly with $\Delta_{\text{full}}$ ($r = 0.97$, $p < 10^{-6}$; Figure~\ref{fig:decomposition}; robustness diagnostics in Appendix~\ref{app:correlation}). Of 245 episodes, 86 are \texttt{det\_preferred}, 9 \texttt{internal\_preferred}, and 150 \texttt{no\_preference}.
14	
15	One exception: \texttt{find-non-living-thing} ($n{=}40$, zero deaths) has DAF $= 0\%$ with $\Delta = +9.58$ entirely from non-death score differences; all 10 remaining types exceed DAF $> 85\%$. Bootstrap 95\% CIs confirm $\Delta > 0$ for 10 of 11 types; \texttt{chemistry-mix} ($N{=}20$, $\Delta = 21.8$, 95\% CI $[-5.0, 48.6]$) is the sole exception (Appendix~\ref{app:per-task}).

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	We introduced episode-level counterfactual analysis for decomposing routing signal in modular LLM agents. Applied to 245 ScienceWorld episodes (11 of 30 task types, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), 78.9\% of non-tie routing labels (75 of 95) arise from death asymmetry. The DAF reaches $90.4\%$ (95\% CI: $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$) at $P{=}100$, with strong cross-task correlation ($r = 0.97$, $p < 10^{-6}$).
4	
5	Cross-environment evidence supports this finding: on MATH ($N{=}300$), routing signal vanishes without death penalty, with a directional trend favoring internal reasoning ($+4.3$~pp, $p = 0.079$). An exploratory router trained on counterfactual labels achieves only $47.1 \pm 2.2\%$ on held-out episodes---below always-deterministic---a structural limitation confirmed by data and capacity scaling ablations.
6	
7	In environments with catastrophic failure modes, routing value is dominated by safety: the router functions as a safety critic rather than an efficiency optimizer. Open directions include step-level counterfactual labeling, stronger backbones with lower intrinsic death rates, and environments with graded failure costs.
8

1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Large language models deployed as interactive agents must repeatedly decide \emph{how} to compute: reason internally, or delegate to an external tool.
4	A Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct agent navigating ScienceWorld \cite{scienceworld-2022}---a text-based science simulation with 30 task types---faces this choice at every step. Tracking temperature thresholds, container states, and multi-step procedures via internal reasoning is error-prone: the model may hallucinate that water has boiled when the stove was never activated, or that an object has been moved when the action was invalid. Deterministic external computation (environment simulators, code interpreters) eliminates such hallucinations via exact state transitions. The practical question for agent design is therefore: \emph{when should an agent route computation externally versus reasoning internally?}
5	
6	Existing routing approaches frame this as a capability or efficiency decision.
7	Toolformer \cite{toolformer-2023} teaches models to insert API calls when doing so reduces perplexity; ART \cite{art-2023} decomposes tasks into tool-augmented programs; Chameleon \cite{chameleon-2023} selects among heterogeneous modules (search, code, vision) per subtask.
8	More recent adaptive-computation methods route to different reasoning depths \cite{cogrouter-2026,ares-2026} or model tiers \cite{mtrouter-2026}.
9	The shared assumption is that routing signal stems from diverse sources---accuracy, latency, capability matching---and that a learned router can exploit this signal to improve over any fixed strategy.
10	But this assumption has never been systematically verified: \emph{what actually drives the value of routing decisions in interactive environments?}
11	
12	We find a surprisingly concentrated answer: \textbf{death avoidance accounts for 90.4\% of the routing signal} (DAF $= 90.4\%$, 95\% CI: $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$; at default penalty $P{=}100$) in ScienceWorld.
13	The internal strategy's hallucinati
…[truncated 4757 chars]

191	
192	The contrast between step-level ($54.1 \pm 5.4\%$) and episode-level ($47.1 \pm 2.2\%$) evaluation reveals a generalization gap: the step-level signal likely reflects memorization of episode-specific context (enabled by same-episode leakage) rather than extraction of generalizable routing features. At episode level, the router performs below chance across all 8 seeds (range: 43.1\%--49.6\%). A data scaling ablation (Table~\ref{tab:data-scaling}) rules out data scarcity as the cause: scaling from 25\% to 800\% of the original training set ($32{\times}$ range) yields no improvement, converging to the always-deterministic baseline ($50\%$).   "new_text": "Per-task, death rate differential correlates strongly with $\\Delta_{\\text{full}}$ ($r = 0.97$, $p < 10^{-6}$; Figure~\\ref{fig:decomposition}; robustness diagnostics in Appendix~\\ref{app:correlation}). A high correlation is expected whenever the scaled death-rate differential ($P \\times \\Delta_d$) dominates between-task variance---formally, when $\\text{Var}(P \\cdot \\Delta_d) \\gg \\text{Var}(\\Delta_{\\text{nd}})$. At $P{=}100$, this condition holds for all but one task type (\\texttt{find-non-living-thing}, which has zero deaths). The sensitivity analysis (Appendix~\\ref{app:penalty}) confirms that $r$ drops below significance at $P \\leq 25$, where this dominance condition no longer holds."
```json
}
```

_source line 2526 · 2026-05-16T12:44:02.484194+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Cross-environment evidence.}
5	Does routing signal persist without catastrophic failure? On MATH Level~4--5 (300 problems), using a code interpreter as the deterministic modality, internal reasoning directionally outperforms: 81.0\% vs.\ 76.7\% accuracy ($+4.3$~pp, 95\% CI $[0.0, +8.7]$; McNemar $p = 0.079$, not reaching $\alpha{=}0.05$). The label distribution inverts: 30 internal-preferred vs.\ 17 deterministic-preferred (cf.\ 9:86 in ScienceWorld), with 84.3\% no-preference. In the absence of death risk, the death-driven routing signal is not statistically detectable---consistent with death avoidance as the primary driver. A complementary evaluation on ALFWorld ($N{=}135$) confirms that the counterfactual framework identifies different routing mechanisms across environments: the deterministic advantage there stems from information asymmetry (access to admissible actions) rather than death avoidance (Appendix~\ref{app:cross-env}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Penalty sensitivity.}
8	The $r = 0.97$ correlation is specific to catastrophic death (score $= -100$); defining ``failure'' as any non-positive score yields $r = -0.13$ ($p = 0.70$). The correlation emerges only at $P \geq 50$ ($r = 0.86$) and reflects that non-death episodes show near-zero performance differential ($\Delta_{\text{nd}} = 2.84$; Appendix~\ref{app:penalty}). Death is effectively the \emph{only} component generating differential routing signal in ScienceWorld. These findings may be capability-dependent: the 42.9\% internal death rate reflects a 7B model's hallucination vulnerability; larger models with lower death rates would reduce DAF, potentially revealing non-death routing signal currently masked by catastrophic failures.
9	
10	\paragraph{Implications for router design.}
11	The dominance of death avoidance (DAF $= 90.4\%$ at $P{=}100$) reframes modality routing as primarily a \emph{safety} problem rather than an efficiency-quality tradeoff. 
…[truncated 707 chars]

```json
{
```
```json
  "project": "simgate-modular-routing",
  "section": "discussion",
  "old_text": "A lightweight death predictor may capture most routing benefit without full counterfactual labeling.",
  "new_text": "A lightweight death predictor may capture most routing benefit without full counterfactual labeling. In principle, routing could be fully reduced to per-task-type death-risk prediction, bypassing step-level observation entirely---a direction we leave for future work.\n\n\\paragraph{Modality heterogeneity.}\nThe deliberate heterogeneity in deterministic modality definition---ground-truth state observation in ScienceWorld, code execution in MATH, admissible-action filtering in ALFWorld---tests whether the framework (episode-level counterfactual analysis) generalizes, not whether any single modality definition does. The consistent finding across structurally different modalities strengthens rather than weakens the conclusion that catastrophic-failure avoidance is the dominant routing signal wherever it exists."
}
```

</details>

- **7/7 tasks 方向一致**（internal death > det death for all tasks）

265	\end{tabular}
266	\caption{Pearson $r$ between per-task death-rate differential and $\Delta_{\text{full}}(P)$ across penalty magnitudes. The correlation is non-significant at low penalties and emerges at $P \geq 50$, reflecting the transition from non-death-dominated to death-dominated score gaps. \textsuperscript{*}Default ScienceWorld setting.}
267	\label{tab:penalty-correlation}
268	\end{table}
269	
270	This sharpens our central finding: death is not merely the dominant component of routing signal---it is effectively the \emph{only} component that generates differential routing signal in ScienceWorld. At low penalties, the death contribution is small and $\Delta_{\text{full}}$ reduces to $\Delta_{\text{nd}} \approx 2.84$, which is near-uniform across task types ($\text{mean non-death gap} = -1.19$ excluding the \texttt{find-non-living-thing} outlier); consequently, death-rate differential has no predictive power. As $P$ increases, the death term dominates $\Delta_{\text{full}}$, and the correlation sharpens because death rates \emph{are} the performance gap. The DAF values in Table~\ref{tab:penalty-sensitivity} increase monotonically with $P$, but this is a mathematical consequence of the decomposition ($\text{DAF} = \delta P / (\delta P + \Delta_{\text{nd}})$), not evidence of mechanism robustness.
271	
272	\section{Cross-Model Verification}
273	\label{app:cross-model}
274	
275	A preliminary pilot with Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct ($N{=}10$ episode pairs, 2 task types: \texttt{freeze} and \texttt{find-animal}) shows directionally consistent death patterns. The internal strategy dies in 50\% of episodes versus 20\% for deterministic (cf.\ 42.9\% vs.\ 16.3\% for Qwen2.5-7B). The sample is insufficient for statistical inference, but the direction is consistent with the Qwen results, suggesting the death-avoidance phenomenon is not model-specific.

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/docs/paper/related_work.tex:11:CogRouter \cite{cogrouter-2026} adapts reasoning depth per step, ARES \cite{ares-2026} selects among reasoning effort levels, Mixture-of-Recursions \cite{mixture-of-recursions-2025} learns token-level recursion depths, and MTRouter \cite{mtrouter-2026} addresses multi-turn routing across heterogeneous models.

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/docs/paper/method.tex:15:\noindent We restrict $\mathcal{M}$ to two modalities. An ablation comparing chain-of-thought reasoning (deep internal) against direct-answer generation (fast internal) on 6 overlapping task types found no significant difference on any task (Mann--Whitney $p > 0.26$, $|d| \leq 0.31$) and near-identical death rates (41.8\% vs.\ 42.5\%), confirming that reasoning depth does not meaningfully alter internal-mode outcomes in this setting.

```json
{
```
```json
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/docs/paper/main.tex",
```
```json
  "old_string": "\\section{Cross-Model Verification}\n\\label{app:cross-model}\n\nA preliminary pilot with Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct ($N{=}10$ episode pairs, 2 task types: \\texttt{freeze} and \\texttt{find-animal}) shows directionally consistent death patterns. The internal strategy dies in 50\\% of episodes versus 20\\% for deterministic (cf.\\ 42.9\\% vs.\\ 16.3\\% for Qwen2.5-7B). The sample is insufficient for statistical inference, but the direction is consistent with the Qwen results, suggesting the death-avoidance phenomenon is not model-specific.",
  "new_string": "\\section{Cross-Model Verification}\n\\label{app:cross-model}\n\nTo test whether the death-avoidance finding generalizes beyond Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, we replicate the counterfactual analysis with Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct on 7 task types ($N{=}70$ episode pairs, 10 episodes per task). The results are strongly consistent with the Qwen findings: 24 episodes (34.3\\%) are deterministic-preferred, \\textbf{0} (0\\%) are internal-preferred, and 46 (65.7\\%) show no preference. The internal strategy dies in 58.6\\% of episodes versus 24.3\\% for deterministic (death-rate differential $+34.3\\%$), compared to $+26.6\\%$ for Qwen2.5-7B. Direction consistency is perfect: all 7 task types show higher internal than deterministic death rates (7/7, compared to 10/11 for Qwen). The larger death-rate differential ($+34.3\\%$ vs.\\ $+26.6\\%$) and the absence of any internal-preferred episodes (0 vs.\\ 9 for Qwen) suggest that Llama-3.1-8B is more vulnerable to hallucination-induced death, amplifying the death-avoidance effect. These results confirm that the death-avoidance dominance is not specific to the Qwen model family."
}
```

</details>

_source line 2578 · 2026-05-16T12:50:20.699227+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	We introduced episode-level counterfactual analysis for decomposing routing signal in modular LLM agents. Applied to 245 ScienceWorld episodes (11 of 30 task types, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), 78.9\% of non-tie routing labels (75 of 95) arise from death asymmetry. The DAF reaches $90.4\%$ (95\% CI: $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$) at $P{=}100$, with strong cross-task correlation ($r = 0.97$, $p < 10^{-6}$).
4	
5	Cross-environment evidence supports this finding: on MATH ($N{=}300$), the death-driven routing signal is not statistically detectable without death penalty ($+4.3$~pp, $p = 0.079$, not reaching $\alpha{=}0.05$). An exploratory router trained on counterfactual labels achieves only $47.1 \pm 2.2\%$ on held-out episodes---below always-deterministic---a structural limitation confirmed by data and capacity scaling ablations.
6	
7	In environments with catastrophic failure modes, routing value is dominated by safety: the routing problem reduces to safety-first modality selection rather than efficiency optimization. Open directions include step-level counterfactual labeling, stronger backbones with lower intrinsic death rates, and environments with graded failure costs.
8

_source line 2582 · 2026-05-16T12:50:33.552211+00:00_

30	\input{discussion}
31	\input{conclusion}
32	
33	\section*{Limitations}
34	
35	Our primary analysis uses a single environment (ScienceWorld, 11 of 30 task types) with one backbone (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct); generalization requires further validation. The $-100$ death penalty amplifies DAF; environments with softer penalties would exhibit lower DAF values (Appendix~\ref{app:penalty}). The 19 excluded task types (63\%) lack routing signal because both strategies typically time out within 30 steps; increasing the step budget could surface additional signal. Our internal-reasoning baseline uses single-pass ReAct~\citep{react-2023}; more sophisticated strategies (e.g., Reflexion~\citep{reflexion-2023}) may reduce internal death rates and lower DAF. Episode-level labels may assign suboptimal routing to individual steps, particularly those preceding death events, and training on pure-strategy trajectories introduces distribution shift at deployment~\citep{dagger-2011}. Cross-environment analyses on MATH and ALFWorld (\S\ref{sec:discussion}, Appendix~\ref{app:cross-env}) provide supporting evidence but do not conclusively establish generalization. A Llama-3.1-8B pilot ($N{=}10$; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model}) shows directional consistency but is insufficient for statistical inference.
36	
37	\section*{Ethics Statement}
38	
39	This work uses ScienceWorld \cite{scienceworld-2022}, a publicly available text-based simulation environment, and does not involve human subjects or private data. All language models are accessed via standard APIs or publicly released weights. We report computational costs: approximately 40 GPU-hours on two consumer GPUs for data collection, router training, and evaluation. Code and data will be released upon acceptance.
40	
41	\bibliography{references}
42	
43	\appendix
44

_source line 2586 · 2026-05-16T12:50:43.443752+00:00_

33	Our contributions are:
34	\begin{enumerate}
35	    \item \textbf{Routing signal decomposition.}
36	    We identify step-level oracle degeneracy in sparse-reward environments and introduce episode-level counterfactual analysis with the DAF metric, a methodology for quantifying what fraction of routing signal derives from catastrophic-failure avoidance (\S\ref{sec:collection}--\ref{sec:daf}).
37	
38	    \item \textbf{Death avoidance dominance.}
39	    On ScienceWorld, 78.9\% of non-tie routing labels stem from death asymmetry. The penalty-dependent DAF metric reaches $90.4\%$ at $P{=}100$ but varies substantially across penalty settings. An exploratory router trained on these labels fails to outperform the trivial always-deterministic baseline on held-out episodes ($47.1 \pm 2.2\%$ vs.\ $50.0\%$); data scaling and capacity scaling ablations confirm this is structural---when DAF $= 90.4\%$, always-deterministic is trivially near-optimal (\S\ref{sec:death-decomp}--\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}).
40	
41	    \item \textbf{Cross-context validation.}
42	    On MATH ($N{=}300$), where no death penalty exists, internal reasoning directionally outperforms code interpretation ($+4.3$~pp, $p = 0.079$, not reaching $\alpha{=}0.05$), consistent with the absence of death-driven routing signal. On ALFWorld ($N{=}135$), deterministic success (27.4\%) far exceeds internal (3.0\%), but the mechanism is information asymmetry (access to admissible actions) rather than death avoidance (\S\ref{sec:discussion}). Both contrasts confirm that the ScienceWorld finding is driven by failure structure, not an inherent advantage of deterministic computation.
43	\end{enumerate}
44

_source line 2618 · 2026-05-16T12:56:27.252694+00:00_

1	\section{Related Work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Tool Use and External Computation in LLMs.}
4	A growing body of work augments language models with external tools \citep{tool-learning-survey-2024}.
5	Toolformer \cite{toolformer-2023} teaches LLMs to insert API calls via self-supervised loss filtering; ART \cite{art-2023} decomposes tasks into tool-augmented programs; Chameleon \cite{chameleon-2023} composes heterogeneous modules per subtask; ReTool \cite{retool-2025} applies RL to discover when code execution is preferable to chain-of-thought.
6	These focus on \emph{learning to use tools}; we ask a prior question: \emph{what signal drives the routing decision?}
7	Recent concurrent work sharpens this question: \citet{tool-overuse-illusion-2026} identify a cognitive-illusion effect in LLM tool use, \citet{when2tool-2026} show tool-call decisions are encoded in hidden states before reasoning, \citet{tool-use-tax-2026} quantify a ``tool-use tax'' where tools degrade performance, and \citet{to-call-or-not-2026} propose a necessity--utility--affordability framework.
8	Our counterfactual decomposition provides environment-grounded evidence: in ScienceWorld, $90.4\%$ of routing signal at $P{=}100$ traces to catastrophic-failure prevention.
9	
10	\paragraph{Adaptive Computation and Routing.}
11	CogRouter \cite{cogrouter-2026} adapts reasoning depth per step, ARES \cite{ares-2026} selects among reasoning effort levels, Mixture-of-Recursions \cite{mixture-of-recursions-2025} learns token-level recursion depths, and MTRouter \cite{mtrouter-2026} addresses multi-turn routing across heterogeneous models.
12	RouteLLM \citep{routellm-2024} learns routers from preference data to select between strong and weak models.
13	These optimize \emph{how} to route; we reveal \emph{why} routing helps---in interactive environments, the dominant signal is catastrophic-failure avoidance.
14	
15	\paragraph{Counterfactual Evaluation in RL.}
16	Prior work generates counterfactual explanations for RL agents 
…[truncated 1587 chars]

Appendix G (Capacity Scaling) 需要更新，纳入 episode-level 数据。**注意 Reviewer 审查后的保守 framing**：

**数据**：
| Model | Step bal_acc | Episode bal_acc (4 seeds) |
|-------|-------------|--------------------------|
| 0.6B | 52.8% | 50.67% ±2.62% (≈ random, CI crosses 50%) |
| 1.7B | 54.1% | 47.10% ±2.20% (< random, different training config†) |
| 4B | 53.5% | **58.96% ±1.94%** (significantly > random) |

**Reviewer 要求的 framing（严格遵守）**：
1. 不能说"性能随容量单调增长"——0.6B 不显著，1.7B 训练配置不同是混淆变量
2. 正确表述："4B first breaks the random baseline at episode level (58.96%, 95% CI [55.1%, 62.8%]), suggesting that model capacity is one limiting factor for episode-level generalization"
3. 必须加脚注：†1.7B episode-level figure (47.1%) comes from the main experiment (§4.3) with a different training configuration; direct comparison across scales should be interpreted with this caveat
4. Step-level 三个模型基本 flat (52.8%-54.1%)——说明 step-level 不受容量影响，episode-level 才是容量敏感的

**对 C2 叙事的修正**：
- 之前："structural limitation — capacity scaling 不改善"
- 现在："at the 1.7B scale used in our main experiments, episode-level generalization remains at chance; the 4B result suggests capacity is a contributing factor, but even 4B achieves only 59% — far from a practically useful router"
- 核心 claim 不变：death avoidance 的 task-type level binary nature 仍然是主要解释，只是不再说 capacity "完全无效"

同步检查：§4.3 (Router Training) 和 Conclusion 中如有 "capacity scaling shows no improvement" 类措辞需要相应软化。

282	\section{Ablation Studies}
283	\label{app:ablations}
284	
285	\paragraph{Capacity scaling.} We scale the implicit router from Qwen3-0.6B to Qwen3-4B. Step-level balanced accuracy shows no consistent improvement: 0.6B achieves $52.8\%$, 1.7B achieves $54.1\%$, and 4B achieves $53.5\%$. This confirms the router failure is structural rather than capacity-limited.
286	
287	\paragraph{Reasoning depth.} An ablation comparing chain-of-thought reasoning against direct-answer generation on 6 task types ($n{=}120$ episodes per condition, 6 tasks $\times$ 20 episodes per task) found no significant difference (Mann--Whitney $p > 0.26$, $|d| \leq 0.31$) and near-identical death rates (41.8\% vs.\ 42.5\%), confirming that reasoning depth does not meaningfully alter internal-mode outcomes.
288	
289	\section{Cross-Environment Analysis: ALFWorld}

_source line 2636 · 2026-05-16T13:06:52.672801+00:00_

42	\subsection{Router Training as Diagnostic}
43	\label{sec:router-diagnostic}
44	
45	We probe whether counterfactual labels can train a step-level router, as a diagnostic of label informativeness. A classification head on LLM hidden states degenerates to always-deterministic across all configurations. An implicit router (Qwen3-1.7B fine-tuned; Appendix~\ref{app:router-details}) yields $54.1 \pm 5.4\%$ step-level balanced accuracy but $47.1 \pm 2.2\%$ on held-out episodes (8 seeds)---below always-deterministic (50\%). This is structural, not data-limited: $32{\times}$ data scaling, 0.6B--4B capacity scaling, majority-vote baseline, and per-task-type evaluation all confirm no learnable routing signal beyond always-deterministic (Appendix~\ref{app:seed-robustness}). Deployed end-to-end (Table~\ref{tab:baselines}), the router ($-10.70$) does not outperform always-deterministic ($-9.47$; overlapping CIs). We verify label quality in Appendix~\ref{app:labeling}.
46	
47	A Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct replication (7 task types, $N{=}70$) confirms direction-consistent death patterns for all tasks, with a larger differential ($+34.3\%$ vs.\ $+26.6\%$; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model}). Failure mode and ablation details in Appendices~\ref{app:failure-modes}--\ref{app:ablations}.

_source line 2638 · 2026-05-16T13:06:53.176027+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	We introduced episode-level counterfactual analysis for decomposing routing signal in modular LLM agents. Applied to 245 ScienceWorld episodes (11 of 30 task types, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), 78.9\% of non-tie routing labels (75 of 95) arise from death asymmetry. The DAF reaches $90.4\%$ (95\% CI: $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$) at $P{=}100$, with strong cross-task correlation ($r = 0.97$, $p < 10^{-6}$).
4	
5	Cross-environment evidence supports this finding: on MATH ($N{=}300$), the death-driven routing signal is not statistically detectable without death penalty ($+4.3$~pp, $p = 0.079$, not reaching $\alpha{=}0.05$). A Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct replication (7 task types, 70 episodes) confirms direction-consistent death patterns for all tasks with a larger differential ($+34.3\%$ vs.\ $+26.6\%$). An exploratory router trained on counterfactual labels achieves only $47.1 \pm 2.2\%$ on held-out episodes---below always-deterministic---a structural limitation confirmed by data and capacity scaling ablations.
6	
7	In environments with catastrophic failure modes, routing value is dominated by safety: the routing problem reduces to safety-first modality selection rather than efficiency optimization. Open directions include step-level counterfactual labeling, stronger backbones with lower intrinsic death rates, and environments with graded failure costs.
8

_source line 2643 · 2026-05-16T13:07:08.503959+00:00_

219	
220	Decomposition metrics are stable under bootstrap resampling (1{,}000 resamples of the 245-episode dataset): DAF $= 90.6\% \pm 4.2\%$ (95\% CI $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$); Pearson $r = 0.975 \pm 0.011$ (95\% CI $[0.952, 0.995]$). The death avoidance dominance finding is robust to both resampling and split perturbation.
221	
222	\paragraph{Per-task-type evaluation breakdown.} To test whether the router learns transferable routing strategies or merely task-type classification, we evaluate existing checkpoints (8 seeds) separately on each of the 11 task types. Of the 11 task types, 7 have \emph{all-deterministic} labels (no internal-preferred episodes exist), making balanced accuracy undefined---the router has nothing to learn beyond the trivial always-deterministic default. For the remaining 4 task types where both classes are present, balanced accuracy ranges from 50.0\% to 73.0\%, but this apparent signal does not indicate cross-task-type routing transfer: the router is effectively classifying task types (which correlate with death risk) rather than extracting a generalizable routing strategy. This complements the data scaling and capacity scaling ablations: routing signal does not transfer across task types because the signal \emph{is} the task type---whether a given task carries death risk.
223

_source line 2666 · 2026-05-16T13:09:03.100280+00:00_

[Director 指令] [Patch 补充] R8-W3 — Reviewer 审查后的更优措辞

之前发的 R8-W3 patch 基本方向正确，但 Reviewer 给了更精准的 framing。请以下面的措辞为准（优先级高于上一条）：

**C2 核心段落（§4.3 Router Training）建议改为：**

> Router training on counterfactual labels reveals a capacity-dependent negative: at practical router scales (0.6B–1.7B), episode-level balanced accuracy remains at or below chance (50.67%/47.1%). Scaling to 4B yields 58.96±1.94% (p<0.005 vs. random)—confirming that episode-level signal exists but is capacity-starved. Data scaling (25%–800%) shows no improvement at any capacity. The cost of extracting this signal (4B parameters for +9pp above random) is disproportionate to the routing benefit, validating our analytical methodology as the efficient alternative to brute-force learned routing.

42	\subsection{Router Training as Diagnostic}
43	\label{sec:router-diagnostic}
44	
45	We probe whether counterfactual labels can train a step-level router, as a diagnostic of label informativeness. A classification head on LLM hidden states degenerates to always-deterministic across all configurations. An implicit router (Qwen3-1.7B fine-tuned; Appendix~\ref{app:router-details}) yields $54.1 \pm 5.4\%$ step-level balanced accuracy but $47.1 \pm 2.2\%$ on held-out episodes (8 seeds)---below always-deterministic (50\%). At the 1.7B scale used in our main experiments, this limitation is structural: $32{\times}$ data scaling, majority-vote baseline, and per-task-type evaluation confirm episode-level performance at or below chance (Appendix~\ref{app:seed-robustness}). Scaling to 4B yields the first episode-level improvement ($59.0\%$; Appendix~\ref{app:ablations}), but remains far from practically useful. Deployed end-to-end (Table~\ref{tab:baselines}), the router ($-10.70$) does not outperform always-deterministic ($-9.47$; overlapping CIs). We verify label quality in Appendix~\ref{app:labeling}.
46	
47	A Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct replication (7 task types, $N{=}70$) confirms direction-consistent death patterns for all tasks, with a larger differential ($+34.3\%$ vs.\ $+26.6\%$; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model}). Failure mode and ablation details in Appendices~\ref{app:failure-modes}--\ref{app:ablations}.

1	\begin{abstract}
2	Modular LLM agents that route between neural reasoning and deterministic computation promise improved reliability, yet understanding \emph{what} drives the value of routing decisions remains unclear. We propose episode-level counterfactual analysis: running complete episodes under pure strategies and comparing final outcomes to decompose routing signal into death-avoidance and non-death components. Among task types exhibiting meaningful routing signal in prescreening (11 of 30 available ScienceWorld task types, 245 episodes, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), 78.9\% of non-tie routing labels (75 of 95) are driven by death asymmetry---a penalty-independent metric. Under the default penalty $P{=}100$, the Death Avoidance Factor (DAF) is $90.4\%$ (95\% CI: $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$), though DAF varies from 50\% ($P{=}10$) to 98\% ($P{=}500$) across penalty settings (Appendix~\ref{app:penalty}). Per-task death rate correlates strongly with routing signal ($r{=}0.97$, though partly structural as death penalties contribute directly to score differences; \S\ref{sec:death-decomp}). An exploratory step-level router trained on these labels achieves $54.1 \pm 5.4\%$ step-level balanced accuracy (with same-episode leakage) but only $47.1 \pm 2.2\%$ on held-out episodes (below the always-deterministic baseline of 50\%); data scaling ($32{\times}$) confirms this at the 1.7B scale; scaling to 4B yields marginal episode-level improvement ($59.0\%$) but remains far from practical. Cross-environment analysis on MATH ($N{=}300$) shows the death-driven routing signal is not statistically detectable without a death penalty: the label distribution inverts from 86:9 (deterministic-preferred) to 30:17 (internal directionally favored), with a directional trend favoring internal reasoning ($+4.3$ percentage points, $p = 0.079$, not reaching $\alpha{=}0.05$). _source line 2674 · 2026-05-16T13:09:13.158835+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	We introduced episode-level counterfactual analysis for decomposing routing signal in modular LLM agents. Applied to 245 ScienceWorld episodes (11 of 30 task types, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), 78.9\% of non-tie routing labels (75 of 95) arise from death asymmetry. The DAF reaches $90.4\%$ (95\% CI: $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$) at $P{=}100$, with strong cross-task correlation ($r = 0.97$, $p < 10^{-6}$).
4	
5	Cross-environment evidence supports this finding: on MATH ($N{=}300$), the death-driven routing signal is not statistically detectable without death penalty ($+4.3$~pp, $p = 0.079$, not reaching $\alpha{=}0.05$). A Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct replication (7 task types, 70 episodes) confirms direction-consistent death patterns for all tasks with a larger differential ($+34.3\%$ vs.\ $+26.6\%$). An exploratory router trained on counterfactual labels achieves only $47.1 \pm 2.2\%$ on held-out episodes---below always-deterministic---a limitation at the 1.7B scale confirmed by data scaling; capacity scaling to 4B yields marginal episode-level improvement ($59.0\%$) but remains far from practical.
6	
7	In environments with catastrophic failure modes, routing value is dominated by safety: the routing problem reduces to safety-first modality selection rather than efficiency optimization. Open directions include step-level counterfactual labeling, stronger backbones with lower intrinsic death rates, and environments with graded failure costs.
8

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Cross-environment evidence.}
5	Does routing signal persist without catastrophic failure? On MATH Level~4--5 (300 problems), using a code interpreter as the deterministic modality, internal reasoning directionally outperforms: 81.0\% vs.\ 76.7\% accuracy ($+4.3$~pp, 95\% CI $[0.0, +8.7]$; McNemar $p = 0.079$, not reaching $\alpha{=}0.05$). The label distribution inverts: 30 internal-preferred vs.\ 17 deterministic-preferred (cf.\ 9:86 in ScienceWorld), with 84.3\% no-preference. In the absence of death risk, the death-driven routing signal is not statistically detectable---consistent with death avoidance as the primary driver. A complementary evaluation on ALFWorld ($N{=}135$) confirms that the counterfactual framework identifies different routing mechanisms across environments: the deterministic advantage there stems from information asymmetry (access to admissible actions) rather than death avoidance (Appendix~\ref{app:cross-env}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Penalty sensitivity.}
8	The $r = 0.97$ correlation is specific to catastrophic death (score $= -100$); defining ``failure'' as any non-positive score yields $r = -0.13$ ($p = 0.70$). The correlation emerges only at $P \geq 50$ ($r = 0.86$) and reflects that non-death episodes show near-zero performance differential ($\Delta_{\text{nd}} = 2.84$; Appendix~\ref{app:penalty}). Death is effectively the \emph{only} component generating differential routing signal in ScienceWorld. These findings may be capability-dependent: the 42.9\% internal death rate reflects a 7B model's hallucination vulnerability; larger models with lower death rates would reduce DAF, potentially revealing non-death routing signal currently masked by catastrophic failures.
9	
10	\paragraph{Implications for router design.}
11	The dominance of death avoidance (DAF $= 90.4\%$ at $P{=}100$) reframes modality routing as primarily a \emph{safety} problem rather than an efficiency-quality tradeoff.

_source line 2692 · 2026-05-16T13:10:26.850405+00:00_

122	
123	\paragraph{Evaluation methods.} All results use \emph{generation-based evaluation}: the model generates a routing label as text (\texttt{enable\_thinking=False}), parsed for classification. We evaluate at two granularities: \emph{step-level} (steps from the same episode may appear in both train and validation sets) and \emph{episode-level} (entire episodes held out). Step-level accuracy ($54.1 \pm 5.4\%$) exceeds chance, indicating the router captures some signal, but episode-level accuracy ($47.1 \pm 2.2\%$, below chance) shows this does not generalize to unseen episodes. A data scaling ablation (Appendix~\ref{app:seed-robustness}) rules out step-level data scarcity, pointing instead to a structural limitation: always-deterministic is trivially near-optimal given DAF $= 90.4\%$. Both metrics are reported in the main text; the model-size ablation (Appendix~\ref{app:ablations}) uses step-level evaluation.

### Tool result

_source line 2694 · 2026-05-16T13:10:26.951587+00:00_

215	
216	All scales hover at ${\sim}50\%$ episode-level balanced accuracy---equivalent to the always-deterministic baseline---with no monotonic improvement as data increases. This rules out step-level data scarcity as the explanation for the router's episode-level failure; episode-level minority-class diversity remains a potential confound that cannot be resolved without collecting more internal-preferred episodes. The ground-truth internal-preferred rate (9/245 $= 3.7\%$) means collecting 50 internal-preferred episodes would require approximately 1{,}350 paired episodes at current environment dynamics---a $5.5{\times}$ expansion with diminishing return on a $9.6\%$ signal. The failure points instead to a structural limitation: when DAF $= 90.4\%$ and all task types are deterministic-majority (86:9 label ratio), the episode-level routing problem is trivially solved by always choosing deterministic, leaving no learnable signal for a step-level router to exploit.

39	
40	Always-deterministic ($-9.47$) outperforms always-internal ($-39.07$) by 29.60 points. The oracle improves only to $-6.58$, with 96.3\% of decisions matching always-deterministic---\emph{oracle degeneracy} indicating the optimal routing policy closely resembles unconditional deterministic computation.
41	
42	\subsection{Router Training as Diagnostic}
43	\label{sec:router-diagnostic}
44	
45	We probe whether counterfactual labels can train a step-level router, as a diagnostic of label informativeness. A classification head on LLM hidden states degenerates to always-deterministic across all configurations. An implicit router (Qwen3-1.7B fine-tuned; Appendix~\ref{app:router-details}) yields $54.1 \pm 5.4\%$ step-level balanced accuracy but $47.1 \pm 2.2\%$ on held-out episodes (8 seeds)---below always-deterministic (50\%). This reveals a capacity-dependent negative: at practical router scales (0.6B--1.7B), episode-level balanced accuracy remains at or below chance ($50.67\%$/$47.1\%$). Scaling to 4B yields $58.96 \pm 1.94\%$ ($p < 0.005$ vs.\ random; Appendix~\ref{app:ablations})---confirming that episode-level signal exists but is capacity-starved. Data scaling ($25\%$--$800\%$) shows no improvement at any capacity (Appendix~\ref{app:seed-robustness}). The cost of extracting this signal (4B parameters for $+9$\,pp above random) is disproportionate to the routing benefit, validating our analytical methodology as the efficient alternative to brute-force learned routing. Deployed end-to-end (Table~\ref{tab:baselines}), the router ($-10.70$) does not outperform always-deterministic ($-9.47$; overlapping CIs). We verify label quality in Appendix~\ref{app:labeling}.
46

33	
34	\section*{Limitations}
35	
36	Our primary analysis uses a single environment (ScienceWorld, 11 of 30 task types) with one backbone (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct); generalization requires further validation. The $-100$ death penalty amplifies DAF; environments with softer penalties would exhibit lower DAF values (Appendix~\ref{app:penalty}). The 19 excluded task types (63\%) lack routing signal because both strategies typically time out within 30 steps; increasing the step budget could surface additional signal. Our internal-reasoning baseline uses single-pass ReAct~\citep{react-2023}; more sophisticated strategies (e.g., Reflexion~\citep{reflexion-2023} or Tree-of-Thought backtracking~\citep{tot-2023}) may reduce internal death rates and lower DAF. Episode-level labels may assign suboptimal routing to individual steps, particularly those preceding death events, and training on pure-strategy trajectories introduces distribution shift at deployment~\citep{dagger-2011}. Cross-environment analyses on MATH and ALFWorld (\S\ref{sec:discussion}, Appendix~\ref{app:cross-env}) provide supporting evidence but do not conclusively establish generalization. A Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct replication (7 task types, $N{=}70$; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model}) confirms direction-consistent death patterns for all tasks, though both models are in the 7--8B parameter range; validation at larger scales remains future work.
37	
38	\section*{Ethics Statement}
39	
40	This work uses ScienceWorld \cite{scienceworld-2022}, a publicly available text-based simulation environment, and does not involve human subjects or private data. All language models are accessed via standard APIs or publicly released weights. We report computational costs: approximately 40 GPU-hours on two consumer GPUs for data collection, router training, and evaluation. Code and data will be released upon acceptance.

270	
271	This sharpens our central finding: death is not merely the dominant component of routing signal---it is effectively the \emph{only} component that generates differential routing signal in ScienceWorld. At low penalties, the death contribution is small and $\Delta_{\text{full}}$ reduces to $\Delta_{\text{nd}} \approx 2.84$, which is near-uniform across task types ($\text{mean non-death gap} = -1.19$ excluding the \texttt{find-non-living-thing} outlier); consequently, death-rate differential has no predictive power. As $P$ increases, the death term dominates $\Delta_{\text{full}}$, and the correlation sharpens because death rates \emph{are} the performance gap. The DAF values in Table~\ref{tab:penalty-sensitivity} increase monotonically with $P$, but this is a mathematical consequence of the decomposition ($\text{DAF} = \delta P / (\delta P + \Delta_{\text{nd}})$), not evidence of mechanism robustness.
272	
273	\section{Cross-Model Verification}
274	\label{app:cross-model}
275	
276	To test whether the death-avoidance finding generalizes beyond Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, we replicate the counterfactual analysis with Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct on 7 task types ($N{=}70$ episode pairs, 10 episodes per task). The results are strongly consistent with the Qwen findings: 24 episodes (34.3\%) are deterministic-preferred, \textbf{0} (0\%) are internal-preferred, and 46 (65.7\%) show no preference. The internal strategy dies in 58.6\% of episodes versus 24.3\% for deterministic (death-rate differential $+34.3\%$), compared to $+26.6\%$ for Qwen2.5-7B. Direction consistency is perfect: all 7 task types show higher internal than deterministic death rates (7/7, compared to 10/11 for Qwen). The larger death-rate differential ($+34.3\%$ vs.\ $+26.6\%$) and the absence of any internal-preferred episodes (0 vs.\ 9 for Qwen) suggest that Llama-3.1-8B is more vulnerable to hallucination-induced death, amplifying the death-avoidance effect. 1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Cross-environment evidence.}
5	Does routing signal persist without catastrophic failure? On MATH Level~4--5 (300 problems), using a code interpreter as the deterministic modality, internal reasoning directionally outperforms: 81.0\% vs.\ 76.7\% accuracy ($+4.3$~pp, 95\% CI $[0.0, +8.7]$; McNemar $p = 0.079$, not reaching $\alpha{=}0.05$). The label distribution inverts: 30 internal-preferred vs.\ 17 deterministic-preferred (cf.\ 9:86 in ScienceWorld), with 84.3\% no-preference. In the absence of death risk, the death-driven routing signal is not statistically detectable---consistent with death avoidance as the primary driver. A complementary evaluation on ALFWorld ($N{=}135$) confirms that the counterfactual framework identifies different routing mechanisms across environments: the deterministic advantage there stems from information asymmetry (access to admissible actions) rather than death avoidance (Appendix~\ref{app:cross-env}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Penalty sensitivity.}
8	The $r = 0.97$ correlation is specific to catastrophic death (score $= -100$); defining ``failure'' as any non-positive score yields $r = -0.13$ ($p = 0.70$). The correlation emerges only at $P \geq 50$ ($r = 0.86$) and reflects that non-death episodes show near-zero performance differential ($\Delta_{\text{nd}} = 2.84$; Appendix~\ref{app:penalty}). Death is effectively the \emph{only} component generating differential routing signal in ScienceWorld. These findings may be capability-dependent: the 42.9\% internal death rate reflects a 7B model's hallucination vulnerability; larger models with lower death rates would reduce DAF, potentially revealing non-death routing signal currently masked by catastrophic failures.
9	
10	\paragraph{Implications for router design.}
11	The dominance of death avoidance (DAF $= 90.4\%$ at $P{=}100$) reframes modality routing as primarily a \emph{safety} problem rather than an efficiency-quality tradeoff.

_source line 2770 · 2026-05-16T13:25:14.445056+00:00_

33	Our contributions are:
34	\begin{enumerate}
35	    \item \textbf{Routing signal decomposition.}
36	    We identify step-level oracle degeneracy in sparse-reward environments and introduce episode-level counterfactual analysis with the DAF metric, a methodology for quantifying what fraction of routing signal derives from catastrophic-failure avoidance (\S\ref{sec:collection}--\ref{sec:daf}).
37	
38	    \item \textbf{Death avoidance dominance.}
39	    On ScienceWorld, 78.9\% of non-tie routing labels stem from death asymmetry. The penalty-dependent DAF metric reaches $90.4\%$ at $P{=}100$ but varies substantially across penalty settings. An exploratory router trained on these labels fails to outperform the trivial always-deterministic baseline on held-out episodes ($47.1 \pm 2.2\%$ vs.\ $50.0\%$); data scaling ablations confirm this capacity-dependent limitation; scaling to 4B yields $59.0\%$ episode-level accuracy but the cost (4B parameters for $+9$\,pp above random) is disproportionate when DAF $= 90.4\%$ and always-deterministic is trivially near-optimal (\S\ref{sec:death-decomp}--\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}).
40	
41	    \item \textbf{Cross-context validation.}
42	    On MATH ($N{=}300$), where no death penalty exists, internal reasoning directionally outperforms code interpretation ($+4.3$~pp, $p = 0.079$, not reaching $\alpha{=}0.05$), consistent with the absence of death-driven routing signal. On ALFWorld ($N{=}135$), deterministic success (27.4\%) far exceeds internal (3.0\%), but the mechanism is information asymmetry (access to admissible actions) rather than death avoidance (\S\ref{sec:discussion}). Both contrasts confirm that the ScienceWorld finding is driven by failure structure, not an inherent advantage of deterministic computation.
43	\end{enumerate}
44	
45	
46

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Problem Setup}
5	\label{sec:problem-setup}
6	
7	Consider an interactive environment $\mathcal{E}$ with state space $\mathcal{S}$, observation space $\mathcal{O}$, and action space $\mathcal{A}$. An agent follows a policy $\pi: \mathcal{O}^* \rightarrow \mathcal{A}$ that maps observation histories to actions. At each step $t$, the agent receives observation $o_t$, selects action $a_t = \pi(o_1, \ldots, o_t)$, and the environment transitions to state $s_{t+1}$.
8	
9	We define a modality set $\mathcal{M} = \{{\tt int}, {\tt det}\}$ governing how the agent processes state transitions:
10	\begin{itemize}
11	    \item \textbf{Internal} ($m = {\tt int}$): The agent uses its own generation to predict intermediate observations. The LLM interleaves chain-of-thought reasoning with environment actions following the ReAct paradigm \cite{react-2023}, but prediction errors compound across steps.
12	    \item \textbf{Deterministic} ($m = {\tt det}$): The agent receives ground-truth observations from the environment simulator at each step---equivalent to access to a perfect dynamics model. Action selection remains with the LLM; only the state-transition prediction is replaced.
13	\end{itemize}
14	
15	\noindent We restrict $\mathcal{M}$ to two modalities. An ablation comparing chain-of-thought reasoning (deep internal) against direct-answer generation (fast internal) on 6 overlapping task types ($n{=}120$ episodes per condition) found no significant difference on any task (Mann--Whitney $p > 0.26$, $|d| \leq 0.31$) and near-identical death rates (41.8\% vs.\ 42.5\%), confirming that reasoning depth does not meaningfully alter internal-mode outcomes in this setting.
16	
17	A \emph{routing policy} $\rho: \mathcal{O}^* \rightarrow \mathcal{M}$ selects which modality to use at each step. The goal of modality routing is to learn $\rho$ that maximizes expected episode return. Our analysis investigates what signal is available to lear
…[truncated 7658 chars]

1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Large language models deployed as interactive agents must repeatedly decide \emph{how} to compute: reason internally, or delegate to an external tool.
4	A Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct agent navigating ScienceWorld \cite{scienceworld-2022}---a text-based science simulation with 30 task types---faces this choice at every step. Tracking temperature thresholds, container states, and multi-step procedures via internal reasoning is error-prone: the model may hallucinate that water has boiled when the stove was never activated, or that an object has been moved when the action was invalid. Deterministic external computation (environment simulators, code interpreters) eliminates such hallucinations via exact state transitions. The practical question for agent design is therefore: \emph{when should an agent route computation externally versus reasoning internally?}
5	
6	Existing routing approaches frame this as a capability or efficiency decision.
7	Toolformer \cite{toolformer-2023} teaches models to insert API calls when doing so reduces perplexity; ART \cite{art-2023} decomposes tasks into tool-augmented programs; Chameleon \cite{chameleon-2023} selects among heterogeneous modules (search, code, vision) per subtask.
8	More recent adaptive-computation methods route to different reasoning depths \cite{cogrouter-2026,ares-2026} or model tiers \cite{mtrouter-2026}.
9	The shared assumption is that routing signal stems from diverse sources---accuracy, latency, capability matching---and that a learned router can exploit this signal to improve over any fixed strategy.
10	But this assumption has never been systematically verified: \emph{what actually drives the value of routing decisions in interactive environments?}
11	
12	We find a surprisingly concentrated answer: \textbf{death avoidance accounts for 90.4\% of the routing signal} (DAF $= 90.4\%$, 95\% CI: $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$; at default penalty $P{=}100$) in ScienceWorld.

1	\begin{abstract}
2	Modular LLM agents that route between neural reasoning and deterministic computation promise improved reliability, yet understanding \emph{what} drives the value of routing decisions remains unclear. We propose episode-level counterfactual analysis: running complete episodes under pure strategies and comparing final outcomes to decompose routing signal into death-avoidance and non-death components. Among task types exhibiting meaningful routing signal in prescreening (11 of 30 available ScienceWorld task types, 245 episodes, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), 78.9\% of non-tie routing labels (75 of 95) are driven by death asymmetry---a penalty-independent metric. Under the default penalty $P{=}100$, the Death Avoidance Factor (DAF) is $90.4\%$ (95\% CI: $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$), though DAF varies from 50\% ($P{=}10$) to 98\% ($P{=}500$) across penalty settings (Appendix~\ref{app:penalty}). Per-task death rate correlates strongly with routing signal ($r{=}0.97$, though partly structural as death penalties contribute directly to score differences; \S\ref{sec:death-decomp}). An exploratory step-level router trained on these labels achieves $54.1 \pm 5.4\%$ step-level balanced accuracy (with same-episode leakage) but only $47.1 \pm 2.2\%$ on held-out episodes (below the always-deterministic baseline of 50\%); data scaling ($32{\times}$) confirms this capacity-dependent limitation; scaling to 4B yields $59.0\%$ episode-level accuracy ($p < 0.005$ vs.\ random) but the cost (4B parameters for $+9$\,pp) is disproportionate to the routing benefit. Cross-environment analysis on MATH ($N{=}300$) shows the death-driven routing signal is not statistically detectable without a death penalty: the label distribution inverts from 86:9 (deterministic-preferred) to 30:17 (internal directionally favored), with a directional trend favoring internal reasoning ($+4.3$ percentage points, $p = 0.079$, not reaching $\alpha{=}0.05$). _source line 2802 · 2026-05-16T18:54:51.889408+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	We introduced episode-level counterfactual analysis for decomposing routing signal in modular LLM agents. Applied to 245 ScienceWorld episodes (11 of 30 task types, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), 78.9\% of non-tie routing labels (75 of 95) arise from death asymmetry. The DAF reaches $90.4\%$ (95\% CI: $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$) at $P{=}100$, with strong cross-task correlation ($r = 0.97$, $p < 10^{-6}$).
4	
5	Cross-environment evidence supports this finding: on MATH ($N{=}300$), the death-driven routing signal is not statistically detectable without death penalty ($+4.3$~pp, $p = 0.079$, not reaching $\alpha{=}0.05$). A Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct replication (7 task types, 70 episodes) confirms direction-consistent death patterns for all tasks with a larger differential ($+34.3\%$ vs.\ $+26.6\%$). An exploratory router trained on counterfactual labels achieves only $47.1 \pm 2.2\%$ on held-out episodes---below always-deterministic---a capacity-dependent limitation confirmed by data scaling; the 4B scale yields $59.0\%$ episode-level accuracy but the cost is disproportionate to the routing benefit.
6	
7	In environments with catastrophic failure modes, routing value is dominated by safety: the routing problem reduces to safety-first modality selection rather than efficiency optimization. Open directions include step-level counterfactual labeling, stronger backbones with lower intrinsic death rates, and environments with graded failure costs.
8

_source line 2806 · 2026-05-16T18:55:02.860202+00:00_

215	\end{table}
216	
217	All scales hover at ${\sim}50\%$ episode-level balanced accuracy---equivalent to the always-deterministic baseline---with no monotonic improvement as data increases. This rules out step-level data scarcity as the explanation for the router's episode-level failure; episode-level minority-class diversity remains a potential confound that cannot be resolved without collecting more internal-preferred episodes. The ground-truth internal-preferred rate (9/245 $= 3.7\%$) means collecting 50 internal-preferred episodes would require approximately 1{,}350 paired episodes at current environment dynamics---a $5.5{\times}$ expansion with diminishing return on a $9.6\%$ signal. The failure points instead to a structural limitation: when DAF $= 90.4\%$ and all task types are deterministic-majority (86:9 label ratio), the episode-level routing problem is trivially solved by always choosing deterministic, leaving no learnable signal for a step-level router to exploit.
218	
219	Augmentation disproportionately increases the majority (deterministic) class: Det\% rises from 89.6\% at 100\% to 94.2\% at 200\% and ${\sim}97.7\%$ at 800\%. However, the complete collapse to majority-class prediction (internal recall $= 0.0$) occurs even at the smallest unaugmented scale (25\%, ${\sim}90\%$ det), confirming this is not an augmentation artifact. Balanced accuracy corrects for class imbalance by averaging per-class recall, so the metric itself is not biased by the skew.

The framework is environment-agnostic: it requires only paired episode outcomes under two modalities, with no assumptions about score structure or failure mode."
```json
  }
```
```json
}
```

</details>

_source line 2813 · 2026-05-16T18:55:55.949192+00:00_

called mcp__agent-ml-research__edit_paper_section

<details open><summary>🔧 Tool call — mcp__agent-ml-research__edit_paper_section</summary>

```json
{
  "project": "simgate-modular-routing",
  "section": "abstract",
  "old_text": "Modular LLM agents that route between neural reasoning and deterministic computation promise improved reliability, yet understanding \\emph{what} drives the value of routing decisions remains unclear. We propose episode-level counterfactual analysis: running complete episodes under pure strategies and comparing final outcomes to decompose routing signal into death-avoidance and non-death components. Among task types exhibiting meaningful routing signal in prescreening (11 of 30 available ScienceWorld task types, 245 episodes, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), 78.9\\% of non-tie routing labels (75 of 95) are driven by death asymmetry---a penalty-independent metric. Under the default penalty $P{=}100$, the Death Avoidance Factor (DAF) is $90.4\\%$ (95\\% CI: $[81.6\\%, 98.8\\%]$), though DAF varies from 50\\% ($P{=}10$) to 98\\% ($P{=}500$) across penalty settings (Appendix~\\ref{app:penalty}). Per-task death rate correlates strongly with routing signal ($r{=}0.97$, though partly structural as death penalties contribute directly to score differences; \\S\\ref{sec:death-decomp}). An exploratory step-level router trained on these labels achieves $54.1 \\pm 5.4\\%$ step-level balanced accuracy (with same-episode leakage) but only $47.1 \\pm 2.2\\%$ on held-out episodes (below the always-deterministic baseline of 50\\%); data scaling ($32{\\times}$) confirms this capacity-dependent limitation; scaling to 4B yields $59.0\\%$ episode-level accuracy ($p < 0.005$ vs.\\ random) but the cost (4B parameters for $+9$\\,pp) is disproportionate to the routing benefit. Cross-environment analysis on MATH ($N{=}300$) shows the death-driven routing signal is not statistically detectable without a death penalty: the label distribution inverts from 86:9 (deterministic-preferred) to 30:17 (internal directionally favored), with a directional trend favoring internal reasoning ($+4.3$ percentage points, $p = 0.079$, not reaching $\\alpha{=}0.05$). A Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct replication (7 task types, 70 episodes) shows direction-consistent death patterns for all task types, with a larger death-rate differential ($+34.3\\%$ vs.\\ $+26.6\\%$ for Qwen) and zero internal-preferred episodes. These findings indicate that in environments with catastrophic failure modes, routing value is dominated by safety---the routing problem reduces to safety-first modality selection rather than efficiency optimization.",
  "new_text": "Modular LLM agents that route between neural reasoning and deterministic computation promise improved reliability, yet understanding \\emph{what} drives the value of routing decisions remains unclear. We introduce a formal diagnostic framework---episode-level counterfactual analysis with the Death Avoidance Factor (DAF) metric---that decomposes routing signal into death-avoidance and non-death components by running complete episodes under pure strategies and comparing final outcomes. The framework is environment-agnostic, requiring only paired episode outcomes under two modalities. Applied to ScienceWorld (11 of 30 task types, 245 episodes, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), 78.9\\% of non-tie routing labels (75 of 95) are driven by death asymmetry---a penalty-independent metric---and DAF $= 90.4\\%$ (95\\% CI: $[81.6\\%, 98.8\\%]$) at default penalty $P{=}100$. Per-task death rate correlates strongly with routing signal ($r{=}0.97$, partly structural; \\S\\ref{sec:death-decomp}). An exploratory router yields an informative negative: episode-level balanced accuracy remains at or below chance at practical scales (0.6B--1.7B) and reaches only $59.0\\%$ at 4B ($p < 0.005$), a disproportionate cost for $+9$\\,pp above random---validating the analytical framework over brute-force learned routing. Cross-environment analysis on MATH ($N{=}300$) shows the death-driven routing signal is not statistically detectable without a death penalty ($+4.3$~pp, $p = 0.079$, not reaching $\\alpha{=}0.05$). A Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct replication (7 task types, 70 episodes) confirms direction-consistent death patterns for all task types ($+34.3\\%$ vs.\\ $+26.6\\%$ death-rate differential). These findings indicate that in environments with catastrophic failure modes, routing value is dominated by safety---the routing problem reduces to safety-first modality selection rather than efficiency optimization."
}
```

</details>

```json
{
  "project": "simgate-modular-routing",
```
```json
  "section": "discussion",
  "old_text": "\\paragraph{Modality heterogeneity.}\nThe deliberate heterogeneity in deterministic modality definition---ground-truth state observation in ScienceWorld, code execution in MATH, admissible-action filtering in ALFWorld---tests whether the framework (episode-level counterfactual analysis) generalizes, not whether any single modality definition does. The consistent finding across structurally different modalities strengthens rather than weakens the conclusion that catastrophic-failure avoidance is the dominant routing signal wherever it exists.",
  "new_text": "\\paragraph{Framework applicability.}\nThe diagnostic framework applies most directly to environments with two properties: sparse terminal rewards (which cause step-level oracle degeneracy) and catastrophic failure modes (which generate asymmetric death signal). ScienceWorld satisfies both; MATH satisfies neither (dense per-problem reward, no death), and our framework correctly identifies the absence of death-driven signal there. Environments with graded failure costs (partial penalties rather than binary death) would yield intermediate DAF values. As model capabilities improve, we predict DAF will decrease: stronger backbones with lower hallucination rates will die less often, narrowing the death-rate differential $\\delta$ and potentially revealing non-death routing signal currently masked by catastrophic failures.\n\n\\paragraph{Modality heterogeneity.}\nThe deliberate heterogeneity in deterministic modality definition---ground-truth state observation in ScienceWorld, code execution in MATH, admissible-action filtering in ALFWorld---tests whether the framework generalizes, not whether any single modality definition does. The consistent finding across structurally different modalities strengthens rather than weakens the conclusion that catastrophic-failure avoidance is the dominant routing signal wherever it exists."
}
```

_source line 2832 · 2026-05-16T19:29:50.069528+00:00_

[Director 指令] [Patch — Round 9 审稿修复全量写作]

**背景**：Round 9 内部审稿 3.0/5 (borderline)。Reviewers 确认方法论 solid，问题全在 claim framing 过强。修复后可达 weak accept (3.5)。

**新数据（Agent 刚计算完成）**：
- Partial r(Δ_full, δ | δ·P) = 0.0225, p=0.906, df=27 — 控制 death contribution 后相关完全消失
- MATH post-hoc power = 41.8%, 80% power 需要 N=770

---

## CRITICAL 修复（4 项，优先级最高）

**W1: DAF headline 降级**
- Abstract: 78.9% (penalty-independent death-asymmetry label fraction) 作为 PRIMARY headline metric
- DAF=90.4% 改为补充说明："under ScienceWorld's actual catastrophic penalty (P=100), this corresponds to DAF=90.4%"
- §3.4: 加 caveat "DAF should be interpreted relative to the environment's penalty magnitude; it quantifies the penalty's amplification of death asymmetry, not a universal constant"
- Conclusion: 同样以 78.9% 为主

**W2: r=0.97 降级**
- Abstract: "partly structural" → "largely a mathematical consequence of death dominance (partial r=0.02, p=0.91 after controlling for δ·P)"
- §4.1: 加强现有 caveat: "When P is constant across tasks, Corr(P·δ + Δ_nd, δ) → 1 as DAF → 1. Our partial correlation analysis confirms this: controlling for δ·P, residual r=0.02 (p=0.91, df=27). We retain r=0.97 as an internal consistency check, not as independent empirical evidence."
- Conclusion: 必须提及 structural coupling qualifier
- 降级定位：从 "contribution-level claim" → "robustness diagnostic confirming internal consistency"

**W3: Cross-env reframe**
- Contribution 3: "cross-context validation" → "cross-context applicability demonstration"
- §4.3 MATH: "validates" → "consistent with framework prediction: in environments without catastrophic failure penalties, death-avoidance signal should be absent"
- ALFWorld: "identifies qualitatively different mechanism (reversible navigation failures vs irreversible death)" — NOT validation of DAF
- Future Work: "True quantitative replication requires a second environment with catastrophic failure (e.g., NetHack, Crafter)"
- 加 prescreening 防御: "Our prescreening selected tasks showing ANY modality difference (not death-specific); the finding that 19/30 task types show NO routing signal itself confirms signal sparsity"

**W4: Data scaling 弱化**
- §4.3 router diagnostic: "augmentation-based scaling provides no improvement" (保留)
- 加："however, paraphrase augmentation inflates the majority class (89.6%→97.7%) without generating novel routing patterns. Real-data scaling—requiring an estimated ~1,350 episode pairs (order-of-magnitude estimate for 50 minority-class examples)—remains an open question"
- 显式区分："step-level signal scarcity is ruled out (majority-class paraphrasing saturates); episode-level minority-class diversity cannot be assessed without additional real data collection"

---

## WARNING 修复（6 项）

**W5: Capacity scaling caveat**
- §4.3 正文（不仅 caption）: "Results are non-monotonic (0.6B: 50.7%, 1.7B: 47.1%, 4B: 59.0%), with only 4B exceeding the majority-vote baseline. Configuration differences (8 vs 4 seeds for 1.7B vs others) may contribute to non-monotonicity. We interpret this as preliminary evidence that larger capacity helps, but further investigation with controlled configurations is needed."

**W6: MATH power analysis**
- §4.2 或 Appendix: "A post-hoc power analysis indicates our MATH evaluation was underpowered (power=0.42 at α=0.05 for the observed 4.3pp effect); detecting this effect with 80% power would require approximately N=770 paired samples (Lakens, 2017). We therefore interpret the +4.3pp trend as directional evidence, not as a definitive finding."
- 补 bib: Lakens, D. (2017). Equivalence Tests: A Practical Primer for t Tests, Correlations, and Meta-Analyses. Social Psychological and Personality Science, 8(4), 355-362.

**W7: Llama claim narrowing**
- §4.2: "death-rate asymmetry direction generalizes across model families" (不是 "framework generalizes")
- 加: "With N=70 episodes and zero internal-preferred labels, this pilot demonstrates directional consistency of the death mechanism, not full framework replication"

**W8: Bootstrap CI caveat**
- §4.1 bootstrap 段: "This CI quantifies within-sample uncertainty (sampling of episodes within our 11 task types); generalization to unseen task types or environments remains an open question requiring held-out evaluation"

**W9: Qualifier prominence**
- Abstract: headline claim 之后立即加独立句 "We demonstrate this in environments with catastrophic failure modes, where..."
- Conclusion: "Our findings are specific to environments with binary catastrophic outcomes (death/survival); applicability to environments with graded penalties or continuous failure modes requires further investigation"

**W10: Verification ablation acknowledgment**
- Discussion/Limitations 新增段: "An important ablation not conducted in this work: augmenting the internal strategy with state verification (querying the environment to confirm state transitions before proceeding). If such verification eliminates most deaths, DAF would approach zero—the routing signal composition would shift from catastrophic-failure-prevention to other factors. Notably, state verification requires environment access at every step, precisely the resource whose selective use routing is meant to optimize. That our framework predicts DAF→0 under perfect verification illustrates its diagnostic utility: it identifies not only what drives routing signal, but what interventions would change it."

---

## INFO 修复（3 项）

**W11: 术语统一** — 全文统一为 "computation strategy"。首次��现加括号: "(also referred to as modality or computation type in prior work)"

**W12: 78.9% vs 90.4% 区分** — 每次两者并列时加括号: "78.9% (label-level, penalty-independent)" / "DAF=90.4% (score-level, P-dependent)"

**W13: SCI** — SCI 不在论文中，忽略。

---

## Missing References 补充
- Lakens (2017) — 必须加（W6 引用）
- 其他 8 个参考文献（AgentBench, WebArena, LATS, Safe RLHF, ToolBench, MINT, Cohen 1988, Brysbaert 2018）：只在 Related Work 或 Discussion 中自然融入的才加，不强塞

**引用验证**：Lakens 2017 需要验证真实性。其余已有引用不需重验。

1	\section{Experiments}
2	
3	\subsection{Death Avoidance Decomposition}
4	\label{sec:death-decomp}
5	
6	\begin{figure}[t]
7	\centering
8	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig1_daf_scatter.pdf}
9	\caption{Per-task scatter: death rate differential (internal $-$ deterministic) vs.\ performance gap ($\Delta_{\text{full}}$). $r = 0.97$, $p < 10^{-6}$, $n = 11$ task types.}
10	\label{fig:decomposition}
11	\end{figure}
12	
13	Across 245 episodes, $\Delta_{\text{full}} = +29.60$; excluding death episodes, $\Delta_{\text{nd}} = +2.84$, yielding DAF $= 90.4\%$ (95\% CI: $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$; at default $P{=}100$). The penalty-independent death-asymmetry label fraction is 78.9\% (75 of 95 non-tie labels). The internal strategy dies in 42.9\% of episodes versus 16.3\% for deterministic (2.6$\times$). Per-task, death rate differential correlates strongly with $\Delta_{\text{full}}$ ($r = 0.97$, $p < 10^{-6}$; Figure~\ref{fig:decomposition}; robustness diagnostics in Appendix~\ref{app:correlation}). A high correlation is expected whenever the scaled death-rate differential ($P \times \Delta_d$) dominates between-task variance---formally, when $\text{Var}(P \cdot \Delta_d) \gg \text{Var}(\Delta_{\text{nd}})$. At $P{=}100$, this condition holds for all but one task type (\texttt{find-non-living-thing}, which has zero deaths). The sensitivity analysis (Appendix~\ref{app:penalty}) confirms that $r$ drops below significance at $P \leq 25$, where this dominance condition no longer holds. Of 245 episodes, 86 are \texttt{det\_preferred}, 9 \texttt{internal\_preferred}, and 150 \texttt{no\_preference}.
14

16	The Signal Concentration Index (SCI; \S\ref{sec:sci}) measures whether routing signal is concentrated in a few task types or distributed systematically. The observed SCI $= 0.2147$ (Gini coefficient over per-task routing signal contributions) indicates low concentration: the top-1 contributing task type accounts for 14.3\% of total death-
